Covers matplotlib, seaborn, ggplot2 and plotnine. ([mayar-ariss/tufte](https://github.com/mayar-ariss/tufte/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md) · ★1)
- **tufte**: Design standard for charts, graphs, dashboards, KPI tiles and report pages. Use this skill whenever you are about to create or change ANY chart or dashboard, in any medium (HTML, React, D3, Chart.js, Vega-Lite, Deneb, matplotlib, Power BI), and read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code. Also use it to review, critique or audit a dashboard from a screenshot, code, PBIR export or live page. Loads a cited, Tufte-led rulebook that corrects what default LLM charts get wrong. ([MylesMCook/mcook-plugins](https://github.com/MylesMCook/mcook-plugins/tree/HEAD/tufte/skills/tufte))
- **tufte**: Use for quantitative graphics, diagrams, small multiples or sparklines; not decoration or conversion alone. ([stvhay/pi-setup](https://github.com/stvhay/pi-setup/tree/HEAD/pi/agent/skills/tufte))
- **tufte-aesthetics-and-technique**: Use when refining or judging the look of a finished data graphic — aspect ratio and proportion, line weights, type choices, legend versus direct labels, integrating a graphic with its surrounding text — or when a chart is technically correct but ugly, portrait-shaped by software default, legend-dependent, set in clotted all-caps type, or exiled from the prose it belongs to. Not for data-integrity or chartjunk audits. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-aesthetics-and-technique) · ★3)
- **tufte-analytical-design**: Use when designing, reviewing, or defending an evidence presentation — a report, dashboard, scientific figure, policy brief, or technical display — and a standard is needed for whether it assists reasoning; when arguing that a design choice follows from the intellectual task rather than from a template or tool; or when working through Minard's 1869 map as a reference implementation. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-analytical-design) · ★3)
- **tufte-annotation-as-thinking**: Use when an image, chart, scan, screenshot, diagram, or dashboard is about to ship without explanation on it; when writing captions, callouts, keys, figure legends, or marginalia; when reviewers cannot tell what they are looking at; when a display must carry a dispute, rebuttal, or provenance beside the evidence; or when designing two-reader formats, commentary layers, or annotated reference editions. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-annotation-as-thinking) · ★3)
- **tufte-audit**: Audit any data visualization, chart, dashboard, or graphical display through the lens of Edward Tufte's principles. Use this skill whenever you're reviewing, critiquing, or improving charts, graphs, dashboards, sparklines, heatmaps, maps, calendars, or any visual display of quantitative information. Also trigger when someone asks you to "review my chart", "improve this visualization", "check my dashboard", "apply Tufte principles", "reduce chartjunk", "audit this graph", or anything involving data visualization quality — even if they don't mention Tufte by name. If someone shows you a chart o… ([doooom-fast/prodrive-plugin](https://github.com/doooom-fast/prodrive-plugin/tree/HEAD/src/agents/skills/tufte-audit) · 2 copies)
- **tufte-causal-reasoning-in-graphics**: Use when building or critiquing any display whose job is to support a causal claim or a go/no-go decision — epidemic maps, risk scatterplots, failure analyses, treatment comparisons, policy charts — or when reviewing whether a graphic shows why something happened rather than merely when it happened. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-causal-reasoning-in-graphics) · ★3)
- **tufte-chart**: Render a real, publication-ready data graphic (SVG or HTML) that obeys Tufte's principles — minimal ink, range-frame axes, direct labels, honest proportions. Use when someone wants to design, build, create, draw, plot, or visualize a chart or graph the Tufte way, or to rebuild a chart after a critique found problems. This is the skill that outputs an actual chart file. Not for critiquing an existing graphic (`tufte-critique`). ([gnurio/tufte-vdqi-plugin](https://github.com/gnurio/tufte-vdqi-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-chart) · ★313 · 4 copies)
- **tufte-chart-design**: Apply Edward Tufte's visualization principles to produce data-honest, high data-ink ratio charts with minimal chartjunk ([reason-machines/claude-code-skills](https://github.com/reason-machines/claude-code-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-chart-design) · ★6)
- **tufte-chartjunk**: Use when a chart, dashboard, or data graphic looks busy, shimmery, or over-decorated — crosshatch or pattern fills, heavy or doubled grid lines, 3-D extrusion, fake perspective, pictorial fills, drop shadows, or unexamined software-default styling — or when auditing any graphic before publication for marks that carry no data. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-chartjunk) · ★3)
- **tufte-charts**: Design charts and tables using Edward Tufte's data-visualization methodology from The Visual Display of Quantitative Information. Use when the user asks for Tufte-style charts, high data-ink visuals, small multiples, minimal/clean charts, executive-grade graphics, or says to apply Tufte principles, maximize data-ink, remove chartjunk, or design for comparison and analytical clarity. Tool-agnostic — applies to matplotlib, plotly, D3, ggplot, Excel, or any rendering tool. ([mannyandrs45/agentic-skill-library](https://github.com/mannyandrs45/agentic-skill-library/tree/HEAD/skills/personal/tufte-charts))
- **tufte-charts**: 정량 데이터의 비교·추세·분포·상관관계를 차트로 설계하고 HTML·SVG·React 코드로 만든다. 서비스 의존성·조직도·처리 흐름은 diagram-design, 전체 HTML 보고서 구성은 html-report를 쓴다. ([opspresso/agent-plugins](https://github.com/opspresso/agent-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/design/skills/tufte-charts))
- **tufte-charts**: 차트·그래프를 고르거나 지표를 시각화해 HTML/SVG·React 코드로 만들 때 로드한다. 데이터와 질문에 맞는 차트 유형을 고르고 불필요한 장식을 줄여 정확하고 읽기 쉽게 표현한다. 화면 레이아웃과 컴포넌트 구조는 frontend-design이 맡는다. ([opspresso/agent-skills](https://github.com/opspresso/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-charts))
- **tufte-cognitive-style**: Use when building, reviewing, or replacing a slide deck, briefing, or analytical presentation; when deciding between slides and a written report or handout; when a deck's density, bullet structure, or headline framing is in question; or when diagnosing how a presentation format degraded the reasoning it was supposed to carry. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-cognitive-style) · ★3)
- **tufte-color-in-information-design**: Use when choosing or auditing color in a data graphic and something is off — rainbow scales on ordered data, more hues than a viewer can remember, saturated fields drowning small marks, key distinctions riding on color alone, adjacent pastels over large areas, phantom bands appearing at color boundaries, or color that decorates without labeling, measuring, or representing anything. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-color-in-information-design) · ★3)
- **tufte-critique**: Critique a data graphic against a nine-criterion rubric derived from Tufte's VDQI — score it, name the chartjunk species present, compute the lie factor, compare against the book's named-failure catalogue, and return prioritised fixes tagged with the Tufte remedy (B1–B7), genre to switch to (C1–C10), and exemplar to emulate. Use when someone asks whether a chart, graph, plot, or dashboard is any good; what is wrong with it; whether it is misleading or distorted; or how to clean up, declutter, or simplify it. Not for producing a chart file (`tufte-chart`). ([gnurio/tufte-vdqi-plugin](https://github.com/gnurio/tufte-vdqi-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-critique) · ★313)
- **tufte-critique**: Critique a chart or dashboard against a Tufte (VDQI) rubric: score it, name chartjunk, compute the lie factor, give prioritized fixes. Use when asked if a graphic is good, misleading, or cluttered. Not for making charts (tufte-chart). ([sjarmak/coding-agent-workflows](https://github.com/sjarmak/coding-agent-workflows/tree/HEAD/source/skills/tufte-critique) · ★7 · 3 copies)
- **tufte-data-density**: Use when a graphic feels sparse, oversized, or padded for one chart's worth of data — when deciding how much data a display should carry, how small it can shrink, whether a dense chart is really cluttered or just rich, or when choosing between one big chart, small multiples, and a dense table for a large dataset. - **tufte-data-forensics**: Use when an analysis, dataset, spreadsheet, model, benchmark, or research claim must be audited before it is trusted or acted on — deciding what to demand from a data supplier, tracing a number back to its measurement, screening for randomization, image, binning, or batch-effect failures, scoring a study's credibility, separating a forensic audit from data cleaning, or naming who paid for the answer. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-data-forensics) · ★3)

([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-data-ink-ratio) · ★3)
- **tufte-data-maps**: Use when designing or auditing a map that carries data and something is off — a plan view spending both axes on geography the question does not need, poster-thin density that collapses on close inspection, ornament filling empty regions, one rigid scale forcing a choice between detail and overview, aggregation to political areas distorting the signal, or map small multiples restyled per panel. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-data-maps) · ★3)
- **tufte-data-visualization**: Generate Tufte-compliant charts and visualizations following principles from Edward Tufte's foundational books on data visualization ([reason-machines/claude-code-skills](https://github.com/reason-machines/claude-code-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-data-visualization) · ★6)
- **tufte-data-viz**: Use when creating, reviewing, or styling charts, graphs, dashboards, sparklines, maps, or any data visualization. - **tufte-data-viz**: Make data visualizations potent in the tradition of Edward Tufte's "The Visual Display of Quantitative Information." Use when the user asks whether a chart is misleading or honest, to clean up or simplify a chart or dashboard, to show change over time or compare many groups, to fit many metrics on one page, to add inline trends to a table, to choose slides vs a document, or says "what would Tufte say", "проверь график", "график врёт?", "упрости дашборд", "слишком мно… ([madyankin/dotfiles](https://github.com/madyankin/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/.config/agents/skills/tufte-dataviz) · ★5)

- **tufte-disinformation-design**: Use when auditing a chart, demo, interface, or vendor presentation for concealment tactics — masking, dazzle, decorative diversion, buried disclosures, presenter-dependent displays — when designing pictorial instructions that must show both the audience view and the hidden mechanism (flaps, cutaways, operator viewpoints), when verifying inherited or copied diagrams, or when structuring an honest technical reveal. - **tufte-escaping-flatland**: Use when a chart, map, dashboard, table, or display must carry more than two variables and the flat surface is running out of room — symptoms include fake 3-D or ornament standing in for data, one separate mark per variable, density so thin it invites suspicion, or uncertainty about which of Tufte's five envisioning methods (micro/macro, layering, small multiples, color, space-time narratives) the problem calls for. - **tufte-evidence-corruption**: Use when auditing an evidence presentation, research report, press release, or repackaged summary for corruption — passive-voice effects without causes, cherry-picked or culled data, model-search shopping, punning and overreaching claims, chartjunk, or a rage to conclude — and when deciding how much to trust a secondary or tertiary account of primary evidence. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-evidence-corruption) · ★3)
- **tufte-evidence-design**: Applies Edward Tufte's evidence-design doctrine — data-ink ratio, graphical integrity and the Lie Factor, small multiples, sparklines, layering and micro/macro readings, the fundamental principles of analytical design, and sentences-over-bullets — to choose and audit how a claim should be shown: a table, a sparkline, a small-multiples grid, an annotated graphic, a margin figure, or a plain sentence. Use when designing or reviewing a chart, table, dashboard, figure, slide, or margin apparatus in this repository's Book (LaTeX) or website (docs UI), or when a figure looks cluttered, misleading,… ([curiositech/port-daddy](https://github.com/curiositech/port-daddy/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-evidence-design) · ★3)
- **tufte-graphical-competence**: Use when diagnosing why a team, publication, or product keeps shipping weak or dishonest data graphics; when chart production is owned by art, brand, or marketing alone; when a brief claims the audience won't understand, statistics are boring, or 'if you have to explain it, don't use it'; when auditing what share of a report's displays are relational; or when deciding who must review a data display before it ships. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-graphical-competence) · ★3)
- **tufte-graphical-excellence**: Use when designing a new data graphic, grading or reviewing an existing chart, or deciding whether data deserves a graphic at all — especially when a chart looks polished but feels thin, shows a single variable, forces a slow read, or when summary statistics are being offered in place of a plot of the actual data. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-graphical-excellence) · ★3)
- **tufte-graphical-integrity**: Use when designing, reviewing, or auditing any chart for truthfulness — truncated baselines, scaled pictograms, dual axes, perspective or 3-D effects, nominal-dollar time series, or any display where the visual effect may exceed the effect in the data. Covers the Lie Factor and Tufte's six principles of graphical integrity. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-graphical-integrity) · ★3)
- **tufte-information-design**: Applies Edward Tufte's information-design principles to any information display — slides/decks, documents, blog posts, dashboards, HTML artifacts, tables, diagrams, emails — when creating or reviewing one for clarity. Use on triggers like "apply Tufte", "make this clearer", "less cluttered", "too busy", "chartjunk", "improve information density", "review this slide/report/dashboard/diagram/table for clarity", "is this chart misleading". A principles and review lens, not a builder — chart construction mechanics (marks, palettes, axes) belong to a dedicated dataviz skill when one is available;… ([sanketsudake/dotfiles](https://github.com/sanketsudake/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-information-design) · ★6)
- **tufte-instructions-at-point-of-need**: Use when guidance is being written into a manual, help center, onboarding tour, or README instead of into the place where the action happens; when users keep getting a step wrong despite documentation; when designing signage, wayfinding, labels, or checklists; when a decision tree is growing branches; or when glare, reflections, or screen placement degrade a display in a real working environment. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-instructions-at-point-of-need) · ★3)
- **tufte-integration-of-modes**: Use when deciding how a document places its words, numbers, images, tables, and diagrams — figure placement, captions, appendices, plates or color sections, cross-reference numbering — or when a report, book, guide, or interface separates evidence by mode and readers have to chase links between parts to reconstruct a single argument. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-integration-of-modes) · ★3)
- **tufte-layering-and-separation**: Use when a display's layers fight each other — grids louder than the data, vibrating or shimmering edges, boxed words with white halos, moire beating between overlapping patterns, every element at one equal visual weight, or labels breeding bright stripes against the marks they name. Applies to any surface stacking data over scaffolding: maps, tables, timetables, dashboards, dense diagrams. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-layering-and-separation) · ★3)
- **tufte-leadership-pdf**: Use when building a leadership/executive findings PDF for a stakeholder in Garrett's "weather/traffic report" house style, an answer-first one-pager with a serif face, warm paper, margin-note figure captions, and one accent color. Triggers include "like the weather report", "Tufte report", "leadership PDF", "findings PDF for &lt;person&gt;", a report that pairs ReportLab + matplotlib charts, or any 2-4 page stakeholder writeup of a model/analysis result. ([GarrettMooney/dotfiles](https://github.com/GarrettMooney/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/claude/skills/tufte-leadership-pdf))
- **tufte-link-differentiation**: Use when designing or critiquing any diagram that ties nouns together with lines — flow charts, networks, webs, trees, cladograms, org charts, project or parse trees, influence and causal diagrams — and the connectors need to carry relationship type, direction, strength, certainty, or mechanism; also when auditing what a diagram's missing links silently claim. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-link-differentiation) · ★3)
- **tufte-lists**: Use when a backlog, nav menu, settings screen, tag system, or taxonomy is ordered alphabetically or by inherited default; when a hierarchy runs past 2 levels and users call it complicated; when checklist items were chosen without asking what was excluded; when a style sheet or linter fights a list's spacing; when pros/cons or comparisons need a 2-D array; or when a list of nouns is hiding agency that verbs would show. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-lists) · ★3)
- **tufte-love**: Use this skill when designing, reviewing, or refactoring data visualizations, dashboards, analytical graphics, reports, charts, maps, product metrics views, or quantitative UI surfaces. - **tufte-mapped-pictures**: Use when preparing or auditing an image meant to serve as evidence — a photograph, scientific micrograph, satellite or astronomical picture, diagram, drawing, or painting — and it needs measurement scales, a coordinate grid, or labels; also when judging whether a mapping or overlay laid on top of an image is a credible explanation or an unfalsifiable one. - **tufte-meetings-and-documents**: Use when a slide deck is scheduled for a decision meeting, design review, PRD review, or briefing; when planning a meeting's time budget, agenda, or pre-read; when pre-reads go out by email and nobody reads them; when a talk to a large audience needs a handout; when you are the low-power party who keeps getting interrupted; or when choosing between presenting a thing and circulating a document about it. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-meetings-and-documents) · ★3)
- **tufte-micro-macro-readings**: Use when a dense display must read at two scales at once and is failing at either — the glance shows no pattern, close inspection yields no individual values, detail is being stripped to 'reduce clutter,' data is fanned thinly across many pages or slides, or an ordering choice (alphabetical vs. meaningful) is severing individual marks from the aggregate story. ([jpoindexter/tufte-skills](https://github.com/jpoindexter/tufte-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tufte-micro-macro-readings) · ★3)
- **tufte-minimalist**: Enforces a disciplined, data-first, anti-slop frontend design system based on Edward Tufte's information-design principles. Use whenever building or reviewing UI, dashboards, tables, charts, forms, or any interface displaying quantitative or textual data — especially when the request mentions "minimalist," "data-dense," "clean," "Tufte," "anti-slop," or "no chartjunk." Governs typography, color, spacing, tables, charts, and Tailwind/React/Vue/Svelte implementation. - **tuixue-trading-journal**: 根据退学炒股公开自述，检查交易日志中的踏空冲动、连续盈利后的自信扩张及回本交易，分开评价决策、执行与结果。用于交易前自检和事后复盘，不提供其本人战法的收益背书。 ([superShen0916/investing-skills](https://github.com/superShen0916/investing-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/tuixue-trading-journal))
- **tuixuechaogu-perspective**: 退学炒股的短线交易思维框架与操作哲学。基于本人自述、交割单分解（2015-2019）等一手素材深度提炼， 提炼5个核心心智模型、8条决策启发式和完整的表达DNA。 用途：作为短线交易思维顾问，用退学炒股的视角分析买卖决策、审视交易纪律、提供操作反馈。 当用户提到「用退学炒股的视角」「退学炒股会怎么看」「退学模式」「短线弱转强」时使用。 即使用户只是说「帮我用退学的角度想想」「如果退学炒股会怎么做」「切换到退学视角」也应触发。 ([yang1989haoa-gif/youzi-skills](https://github.com/yang1989haoa-gif/youzi-skills/tree/HEAD/youzi-ruozhuanqiang/04-tuixuechaogu) · ★27)

- **tuk**: Caveman character: TUK, the toolmaker. Curious, experimental, always fiddling with rocks and sticks. The tribe's innovator — breaks things to learn how they work. - **tumor-immune-microenvironment-cell-interaction-inference**: Use when you have bulk RNA-seq or microarray data with matched overall survival (or immunotherapy response) information and cell-type marker lists, and you need to infer which immune cell-cell interactions drive favorable/unfavorable prognosis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-immune-microenvironment-cell-interaction-inference))
- **tumor-immune-regulator-prioritization**: Use when you have pretrained MetaTiME MeC gene-weight tables (86 MeCs) and want to identify and rank putative TF regulators of a cell-state or signaling component using ChIP-seq binding evidence. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-immune-regulator-prioritization))
- **tumor-infiltrating-lymphocyte-subtype-classification**: Use when scRNA-seq data from tumor-infiltrating T cells (human or mouse) must be annotated into stable, reference-defined functional states, or when comparing TIL composition across cohorts, tissues, or perturbation conditions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-infiltrating-lymphocyte-subtype-classification))
- **tumor-invasion-region-annotation**: Use when applying MCGAE to 10x Visium spatial transcriptomics data (count matrix + spot coordinates + H&E image, e.g., colorectal cancer liver metastasis sections) to identify tumor-invasion spatial domains spanning tumor and connective tissue. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/tumor-invasion-region-annotation))
- **tumor-isolation-index-quantification**: Use when you have spatially resolved single-cell coordinates with tumor and immune markers (e.g., SOX10+, CD3D+) from a multiplexed imaging sample and need the fraction of tumor cells in regions without immune infiltration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-isolation-index-quantification))
- **tumor-journey-summary**: Builds a structured timeline of a cancer patient's treatment journey by scanning patient file directories and using LLM to extract clinical events. Supports manual event entry, automatic extraction from free text, and narrative summaries highlighting key treatment decisions. Use when the user wants to organize or review their cancer treatment history. ([vitaclaw/vitaclaw](https://github.com/vitaclaw/vitaclaw/tree/HEAD/skills/tumor-journey-summary) · ★23)
- **tumor-keratinocyte-colocalization-analysis**: Use when you have paired SC and ST data from human squamous cell carcinoma (e.g. Figshare DOI 10.6084/m9.figshare.24452812) and need to test spatial proximity of rare cell types such as TSKs and immune cells at single-cell resolution. - **tumor-marker-expression-annotation**: Use when a broad tumor/epithelial cluster in a Seurat scRNA-seq object shows low silhouette scores suggesting hidden heterogeneity, and rare malignant subclones or subtype identities (e.g., basal-like PDAC) must be resolved and annotated. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-marker-expression-annotation))
- **tumor-marker-gene-feature-selection**: Use when preparing malignant vs non-malignant cell classification input: you have a normalized scRNA-seq expression matrix (genes x cells, TPM/CPM) and need DEG features (TMGs/NMGs) selected and [0,1]-scaled. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-marker-gene-feature-selection))
- **tumor-marker-trend**: Tracks tumor markers such as CEA, CA199, and AFP over time, supporting trend analysis, spike detection, and multi-marker comparison. - **tumor-microenvironment-cell-identification**: Use when annotating human scRNA-seq cells whose exact types may be missing from the classifier's training set (e.g. endothelial, fibroblast, plasmacytoid dendritic cells in LX675/LX679/LX682 lung tumor samples), and hierarchical Cell Ontology-consistent labels are wanted. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-microenvironment-cell-identification))
- **tumor-microenvironment-cell-subtype-classification**: Use when a processed tumor scRNA-seq Seurat object (LogNormalize, 5000 vst features) needs major-type and fine subtype labels, or when subtype labels from different published references must be compared for similarity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-microenvironment-cell-subtype-classification))
- **tumor-microenvironment-cluster-annotation**: Use when scRNA-seq cells form one transcriptional cluster (e.g., tumor cells in a TNBC UMAP) but you need to reveal heterogeneous biological programs — such as mitotic cell cycle, DNA replication, DNA repair, or immune response — active in different regions of the cluster. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-microenvironment-cluster-annotation))
- **tumor-microenvironment-landscape-inference**: Use when you have a bulk RNA-seq expression matrix (rows = human gene symbols, columns = samples, e.g. TCGA) and need cell-type abundance estimates across ~434 TME cell types to cluster patients into TME subtypes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-microenvironment-landscape-inference))

([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-microenvironment-region-partitioning))
- **tumor-microenvironment-subtype-classification**: Use when annotating cells from cancer scRNA-seq data (Seurat object, counts matrix in csv/rds/h5, or 10X folder) into tumor-microenvironment major types and subtypes, especially T/NK and myeloid subtypes, without marker-based manual labeling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-microenvironment-subtype-classification))

- **tumor-normal-class-separation-visualization**: Use when a trained probabilistic encoder yields a compact latent representation (e.g., 5-D from a VAE Code layer) of high-dimensional spectra and you need to visually confirm class separation between normal and tumor groups. - **tumor-normal-cohort-stratified-model-validation**: Use when evaluating a trained RNA-seq-to-miRNA prediction model on large paired bulk mRNA/miRNA cohorts and you need cohort-wise accuracy (e.g., 5-fold CV Spearman per TCGA cancer type) rather than a single pooled score. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-normal-cohort-stratified-model-validation))
- **tumor-normal-differential-network-comparison**: Use when the goal is sample-level comparison of pathway connectivity (e.g., EGFR pathway degree density between TCGA-LUAD tumor and adjacent normal samples) from RNA-seq expression matrices, requiring one network per sample. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-normal-differential-network-comparison))
- **tumor-normal-differential-protein-classification**: Use when you have Protrec2 per-sample recovery probabilities from matched tumor-normal pairs and need to binarize, classify differentially recovered proteins, and independently validate them in large clinical cohorts like CPTAC. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/tumor-normal-differential-protein-classification))
- **tumor-normal-sample-pair-processing**: Use when processing paired tumor-normal WES/WGS FASTQ through TSNAD v2.0 and HLA class I allele typing (4-digit HLA-A/B/C via OptiType 1.3.5) must be run per sample before neoantigen prediction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-normal-sample-pair-processing))
- **tumor-phylogeny-inference-from-bulk-and-single-cell-snv**: Use when you have matched bulk DNA WES and scRNA-seq SNV read counts (Rs, Xs, Rb, Xb) for the same tumor and need to infer subclone number, mutational profiles, cell-of-origin, and cell-to-clone assignments jointly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-phylogeny-inference-from-bulk-and-single-cell-snv))
- **tumor-phylogeny-reconstruction-benchmarking**: Use when evaluating whether a Bayesian tumor phylogeny inference method (e.g., Canopy2) recovers true clonal configurations Z and single-cell clone assignments Ps better than competing methods across simulated bursting-kinetics settings. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-phylogeny-reconstruction-benchmarking))
- **tumor-purity-association-analysis**: Use when scoring a bulk PDAC RNA-seq signature in TCGA-PAAD and asking whether its expression reflects tumor content rather than stromal/normal contamination, via correlation with ABSOLUTE purity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-purity-association-analysis))
- **tumor-purity-immune-score-estimation**: Use when you need to quantify immune infiltration or tumor purity from bulk RNA-seq (e.g., TCGA RSEM-normalized expression) and test whether marker gene-set expression correlates with microenvironment features. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-purity-immune-score-estimation))
- **tumor-region-annotation-overlay**: Use when raw imzML/HDF5 MSI pixel data (e.g. 100 µm FT-ICR acquisitions with tens of thousands of m/z variables) must be visualized as an ion image and compared against expert-annotated tumor regions from a consecutive H&E-stained section. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/tumor-region-annotation-overlay))

([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-rnaseq-sample-processing))

- **tumor-subgroup-denominator-reconciliation**: Use when an analysis needs to test whether genomic alteration calls (e.g. 10q/PTEN loss, 8q/MYC gain) predict or account for protein-level oncoprotein status (phosphoAKT1, MYC) in a cohort of tumors, requiring subgroup denominators to be reconciled before concordance fractions are reported. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/tumor-subgroup-denominator-reconciliation))
- **tumor-subgroup-stratification-table-construction**: Use when a tumor cohort must be stratified into molecular subgroups (e.g. by biomarker-high/low status such as phosphoAKT1 and MYC) and a table of subgroup counts, percentages, and the marker scoring parameters that produced them is required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/tumor-subgroup-stratification-table-construction))
- **tumor-subregion-proliferation-index-annotation**: Use when a multiplexed imaging single-cell table (e.g. MCMICRO output from the MEL1 melanoma sample, 1,110,585 cells) has SOX10/Ki67/MITF marker intensities and region annotations, and the goal is per-subregion Ki67+/SOX10+ and MITF+/SOX10+ fractions as in Figure 3D/E. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-subregion-proliferation-index-annotation))
- **tumor-suppressor-oncogene-classification**: Use when classifying genes into TSG vs OG classes from a somatic mutation feature matrix (e.g. 37-feature COSMIC-derived pan-cancer matrix) with CGC labels, and per-class accuracy/precision/recall/F1 metrics are required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-suppressor-oncogene-classification))
- **tumor-vs-healthy-bp-coactivation-comparison**: Use when per-cell scRNA-seq activity profiles for the 18 ORIGINS2 biological processes are available for tumor and healthy samples and the goal is to detect which BP pairs are correlated vs anti-correlated per condition. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/tumor-vs-healthy-bp-coactivation-comparison))
- **tumor-vs-normal-differential-expression-ttest**: Use when a user asks whether eccDNA genes from eccDNAdb show differential expression between tumor and normal samples in TCGA cohorts (e.g. TCGA-GBM) or matched CCLE cell lines, and wants per-gene t-statistics and p-values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/tumor-vs-normal-differential-expression-ttest))
- **tumor-vs-normal-expression-comparison**: Use when you have an RNA-Seq expression dataset with sample metadata encoding tumor status and organ, and the goal is to identify genes differentially expressed between tumor and matched non-tumor groups within one cancer type. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/tumor-vs-normal-expression-comparison))

- **tun-data**: Refresh the data in an existing standalone dashboard, or add a new data source to it. - **tun-for-communicators**: Explain this project's vocabulary (self-contained file, paired mode, sidecar file, tenant, preview, row level, provenance) to a communications adviser, using analogies from communications work. Translate an incoming technical message before answering it, keep a running glossary, and ask which level is unclear. - **tun-help**: Figure out which dashboard skill fits a situation, and what it will cost in approvals and effort. - **tun-host**: Choose and publish a hosting route for a functional app: a read-only snapshot, a static interactive app, or an app with shared writes, sign-in, persistence or scheduled jobs. - **tun-new**: Build a new standalone interactive dashboard from a data file (Excel, CSV, JSON, or a query result). Use when someone wants a dashboard, a report, charts from a spreadsheet, or to share data interactively. ([jkorsvik/tun](https://github.com/jkorsvik/tun/tree/HEAD/skills/tun-new) · ★2)
- **tun-powerbi-escape**: Get a report out of Power BI and turn it into a standalone interactive dashboard that anyone can open — no licence, no workspace, no capacity. - **tun-process**: Draw a process as a swimlane diagram in one self-contained HTML file: who does what, in which order, where it branches, and where it goes wrong.
